# Planned illustrations

346 unique illustrations, generated by `tools/art_list.py` from the catalog, subject plan, briefs and `config/art_style.json`. Uniqueness is enforced by `tools/art_catalog.py`. Status marks: ✓ approved, ◐ received or proposed and awaiting review, ✗ sent back for a redo, ★ owner-authored prompt.

## Shared template

Every prompt has these sections, in order:
1. title;
2. introduction;
3. format;
4. composition;
5. subject details;
6. activity;
7. source text (verbatim flavour);
8. style and atmosphere;
9. final requirements;
10. style references.

The lines below are shared. Each entry lists only its own lines.

**Introduction:** Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of {identity}, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.

**Format, scenes:** {aspect_words} composition. Pure black ink on warm ivory-white paper, with no colour, lettering, borders, or decorative framing. The illustration must have a natural, irregular, hand-drawn white vignette around all four edges. The artwork gradually dissolves into blank ivory-white paper through increasingly sparse pen strokes, broken outlines and diminishing hatching. Leave approximately 5-8% clean white margin around the perimeter, with an additional organic transition zone. Avoid digital blur, artificial feathering, or hard rectangular cropping. Keep crisp line and strong contrast at the focal point.

**Format, figures:** {aspect_words} composition. Pure black ink on warm ivory-white paper, with no colour, lettering, borders, or decorative framing. A single subject drawn whole, isolated on blank ivory-white paper: only a small patch of ground beneath it and its cast shadow, which fade out in a few sparse strokes. No background scenery. Leave at least 8% clean white margin on every side; nothing touches or is cropped by the image edge. The silhouette must read clearly when printed about two inches wide.

**Format, still lifes:** {aspect_words} composition. Pure black ink on warm ivory-white paper, with no colour, lettering, borders, or decorative framing. A still-life arrangement isolated on blank ivory-white paper, resting on a small surface that fades out in a few sparse strokes. No figure and no background scenery. Leave at least 8% clean white margin on every side; nothing touches or is cropped by the image edge.

**Style, scenes:** Ink it gritty and bold, like old-school rulebook art: strong brush contours of uneven weight, large shapes of solid black for shadow, night and depth, and rough, scratchy hatching and dry-brush drag where light turns into shadow, with the bite of a woodcut. High contrast: let solid black and open white paper carry the picture, with few in-between textures. Visible human marks are welcome: broken lines, dry-brush streaks, a few flicked ink spatters. Put the deepest blacks and sharpest contrast at the focal point and let distance thin to simpler, lighter line. No stipple fields, no uniform micro-texture, no tonal haze, no smooth digital rendering. Accurate perspective, believable construction and coherent geography; the world shows withered beauty: once-grand things rusted, cracked, overgrown or hollowed by age. It must look drawn by hand with brush and ink, not generated or rendered.

**Style, figures and still lifes:** Ink it like gritty old-school rulebook monster and character art: a bold, rough brush contour of uneven weight, heavy solid-black shadow shapes, and scratchy hatching or dry-brush drag where form turns from light into shadow. High contrast, with few in-between textures; small human imperfections such as broken lines and dry-brush streaks are welcome. It reads as a strong silhouette first and detail second: no stippled skin, no uniform micro-texture, no over-rendered surfaces. Light falls from the upper left. Believable anatomy, armour construction, weapon grips and materials, worn, scarred and weathered. It must look drawn by hand with brush, pen and ink, not generated or rendered.

**Final, scenes:** Avoid photorealism, digital-render polish, clean vector-like line, uniform machine texture, stipple fields, painterly shading, smooth gradients, grey wash or grey ink tones, airbrush, colour, typography, readable runes, watermarks, signatures, modern objects, artificial symmetry and competing focal points. The finished artwork should be gritty and high-contrast and print crisply in pure black ink, with an organically fading white vignette, like original ink art in an old-school fantasy rulebook.

**Final, figures and still lifes:** Avoid photorealism, digital-render polish, clean vector-like line, stippled or noisy texture, painterly shading, smooth gradients, grey wash or grey ink tones, airbrush, colour, typography, readable runes, watermarks, signatures, modern objects, cartoon or anime proportions and cropped limbs. The finished artwork should be gritty, bold and readable at small size and print crisply in pure black ink, like original ink art in an old-school fantasy rulebook.

**References:** The attached reference images show the target ink style. Match their bold, rough contours, heavy solid blacks, scratchy hatching, dry-brush texture, contrast and finish. Some are centuries-old etchings or woodcuts: take only their line, blacks and finish, never their period costume. Do not copy their subjects or compositions, and draw in pure black ink only.

## Cover (1)

Printed 4.8 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: Cinematic establishing scene with one clear focal group; open white sky in the upper third reserved for the typeset title; strong black foreground framing. Composition reads as a vignette: the drawing is densest at its focal point and thins out into white paper toward irregular edges; no rectangular edge, no frame.

### 1. Front Cover ✓

`ART-COVER-002`

```text
FRONT COVER - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of the Recommended First Party of Heraldry Skirmish (Human Fighter, Elf Ranger, Dwarf Cleric, Halfling Rogue), four Heroes on an expedition into the monster-haunted ruins of the Dying Lands while a rival party works the same ground, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
A four-Hero adventuring party — Human Fighter, Elf Ranger, Dwarf Cleric, Halfling Rogue — advancing through a rain-soaked ruined shrine; a rival party's banner across the ruin; a Monster silhouette in the treeline.
Low three-quarter view from ground level; the four-Hero party is small in the lower third, advancing between rain-slick flagstones. A broken shrine arch frames the scene on one side and the upper half of the picture is open ruled sky, left empty for the typeset title.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Broken gothic shrine arch with a missing keystone and a headless saint in a wall niche
* Rain drawn as sparse fine diagonal strokes, puddles on the flagstones reflecting the arch
* Human Fighter in a ring shirt with shortsword and small round targe at the front, Elf Ranger in a leather coat with a shortbow, broad Dwarf Cleric in a ring shirt with a mace and small round targe, small Halfling Rogue in a leather coat with a rapier at the hip slipping along the wall edge
* A rival party's tattered banner on a pole planted across the rubble, no readable device
* Fallen columns, toppled statue fragments and moss on the stones
* Treeline at the far edge with one Monster silhouette standing between the trunks, shape only
* Lantern hung on the Ranger's pack, soaked cloaks, packs and bedrolls
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* The four Heroes advance in a loose staggered file, weapons half-drawn, looking toward the banner
* Halfling Rogue crouches at the edge peering at the banner pole
* Monster silhouette stands motionless in the trees, unseen by the party
Wary, rain-heavy and austere; adventurers small against withered grandeur.
Do not draw:
* Do not render the title or any lettering on banners.
* Do not detail the Monster: a dark silhouette only.
* Do not make the party large or heroic-posed; no sunburst or glow.
* Do not give the rival banner a heraldic device that reads as a symbol.
* No plate armour, longbow or staff on the four Heroes.
```

## Chapter openers (11)

```text
CHAPTER 1 OPENER - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of the Dying Lands as a whole, seen from a broken mountain pass: forest on one flank, frozen peaks on the other, and the dead empire's ruined cities sprawled on the eastern horizon, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
The Dying Lands seen from a broken mountain pass: a cracked imperial road winding down toward the skeletal towers of a dead city on the horizon, forest to one side and frozen peaks to the other; a small party with a banner pauses on the road in the foreground.
Wide vista from a high pass; a cracked imperial road winds down from the lower foreground toward the skeletal towers of a dead city on the horizon. A small party with a banner pauses at lower left, road leading the eye to the city.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Straight, patient imperial road paved in broken slabs, with weeds and a leaning milestone half-swallowed by roots
* Forest mass pressing in on one side, drawn as a dense hatched canopy with solid-black depths
* Frozen peaks with fine sparse snow-line hatching on the other side
* Distant dead city of snapped spires, collapsed domes and empty arches in thin fine lines
* Fractured pass rocks and a cairn in the foreground
* Wisps of smoke or ash thinly ruled over the far city
* Sparse horizontal cloud strokes
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* Party of four stands on the road at lower left, one holding a banner on a pole, looking down toward the city
* No other figures
Vast, hushed and fatalistic; the world laid out like a wound.
Do not draw:
* Do not repeat the cover composition or the Greythorn bastion portrait; no walled town visible.
* No large heroic party and no legible device on the banner.
* Do not include any monsters or dragons in the sky.
* No panorama of all six climates.
```

## World sections (40)

Printed 3.3 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: Small establishing scene that captures the section's subject. Composition reads as a vignette: the drawing is densest at its focal point and thins out into white paper toward irregular edges; no rectangular edge, no frame.

### 1. 1.2 The Grim World ✓

`ART-WOR-1-2-THE-GRIM-WORLD`

```text
1.2 THE GRIM WORLD - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of the Grim World: kings, bishops and warlords raise banners over land they can barely hold, and peasants bear the cost, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
Mid-distance view of a burnt farmstead yard with two warlords' banners planted on opposite sides; the tax cart and armed men fill the yard while a peasant family flees down a lane to one side.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Blackened timber farmhouse with a fallen roof and a standing chimney
* Two tall banner poles on either side of the yard with tattered banners of different cut (no legible devices)
* Tax collector's cart with strongbox, ledger and a seated clerk
* Armed men in mismatched gear holding spears, one counting sacks
* Burnt barn, scattered tools and a dead cow or ox
* Fleeing peasant family on the lane with bundles, a child carried, a dog

HUMAN ACTIVITY
* Armed men load the cart and loom over the yard
* The family flees down the lane, looking back
* Clerk records the taking
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"The world of Heraldry Skirmish is a realm of old faiths, broken kingdoms, hungry roads, and cold bargains. Kings, princes, bishops, and warlords still raise banners over land they can barely hold. They fight over relics, tolls, doctrine, trade roads, salt, iron, and pride. Their victories are measured in burnt farms and new taxes. Their defeats are carried by peasants who never chose a side."
Bitter, mundane cruelty of petty war.
Do not draw:
* No monsters; the cruelty here is human.
* Do not show violence against the family.
* No readable heraldry on banners.
* Not an abandoned monster-held village street.
```

### 3. 1.3.2 The Six Climates of Ruin ✓

`ART-WOR-1-3-2-THE-SIX-CLIMATES-OF-RUIN`

```text
1.3.2 THE SIX CLIMATES OF RUIN - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of the shape of the Dying Lands: broken mountains down the spine, the dead empire in the east, the great forest in the west, ice in the north, dunes in the south and swamps in the south-east, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
A sweeping panorama from the spine of the broken mountains with all six climates under one sky: forest to the west, dead city to the east, ice to the north, dunes to the south and drowned fen to the south-east, with dark cavern mouths in the mountainside below; a lone surveyor with a staff stands in the foreground.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Jagged mountain crest with a cairn and a rock ledge foreground
* Western forest as dense hatched mass running to a pale coast
* Eastern city skyline of snapped towers and domes in fine lines
* Northern ice fields with glacial walls and thin hatching
* Southern dunes with tomb-teeth along the horizon
* South-eastern fen of reeds, black pools and drowned trees in sparse fine lines
* Dark cavern mouths and old mine adits in the mountain flank below the ledge (the Undermarches)
* Imperial roads as straight lines radiating from the east across the lands
* Surveyor with a staff, a rolled map and a leather satchel
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* The surveyor stands at the cairn looking across the lands, one hand raised to shade eyes
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Beyond the walls, land has become allegiance. Expedition ledgers still say forest, city, cavern, ice, desert, and swamp. Survivors know better. They use older names. The shape of the world is plain enough to anyone who has walked it. The broken mountains run down its spine. The dead empire and its ruined cities sprawl across the eastern heart. The great forest spreads west to the coast, the ice locks the north, the dunes burn in the south beyond the mountain passes, and the swamps drown the south-east where the rivers give out at the sea. The old imperial roads still join all of it, straight and patient, running out from the Ashlands to every climate that survived. A dry label cannot warn a traveller that a road has memory, a court still sits without lungs, or a pool of black water may be listening."
Immense, quiet and unsettled.
Do not draw:
* Not a mountain-pass vista with a party and a single road toward one city.
* No dragons or creatures.
* No map lettering.
* No hard compass-rose graphic.
```

```text
SUBJECT DETAILS

Now they are the great powers of what remains."
Hard survival bought with others' lives.
```

### 9. Rivals at the chest ✓

`ART-END-WHAT-IS-HERALDRY-SKIRMISH`

```text
RIVALS AT THE CHEST - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of Two rival adventurers kneel at either end of the same iron-bound treasure chest in a monster-haunted ruin, each with one hand on its handle, both looking up at a clawed Monster arm reaching over the wall behind it: the competitive race for treasure in miniature, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
Closing tailpiece at the foot of a page, printed about 2.6 to 3.3 in wide and as short as 1.6 in: one clear focal subject with a strong silhouette in the middle of the frame, generous open white paper around it, and every edge dissolving into the paper in sparse broken strokes. Straight side view at kneeling height, symmetrical and calm: the chest sits at the centre on cracked flagstones; the Human kneels on one knee at its left end facing right, the Elf kneels on one knee at its right end facing left; a knee-high broken wall stub runs behind the chest, and over its top reaches a single long clawed arm, shape only.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Iron-bound wooden treasure chest, lid shut, one ring handle on each short end
* Human in a battered ring shirt: near hand gripping the left ring handle with the arm straight and relaxed, far hand resting on the hilt of a shortsword still in its scabbard at the hip
* Elf in a leather coat: near hand gripping the right ring handle with the arm straight and relaxed, far hand holding a dagger low at the side, point down
* Both heads turned up toward the clawed arm above the wall
* Broken wall stub with moss; the clawed arm drawn in solid black
* A few plain coins spilled from a split seam
* Cracked flagstones fading out in a few strokes
Must be shown exactly:
* Exactly two rival figures and one chest; the Monster shows only as one clawed arm and shoulder
* Every figure has exactly two arms, two hands and two legs; shoulders, elbows, wrists and knees bend only the natural way, no limb twists behind the body or merges into another figure
* Every weapon and tool is one whole, correctly built object that is gripped by a visible hand, sheathed, or resting on a surface; nothing floats in the air
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* Both rivals freeze, each gripping their own handle, and look up at the arm
Greed and dread in the same breath; the richest party is the one that bleeds.
Do not draw:
* No lettering, numbers, dice, game tokens, or rulebook pages.
* Do not repeat the chapter 2 sighting across a fog valley or the cover's shrine arch.
* No full Monster figure, face or portrait.
* No banners or heraldic devices.
* No gore.
```

```text
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of a Hero at the foot of a cellar stair lets a lit torch fall from one hand while the other hand draws a sword, as shapes stir in the dark ahead, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
Closing tailpiece at the foot of a page, printed about 2.6 to 3.3 in wide and as short as 1.6 in: one clear focal subject with a strong silhouette in the middle of the frame, generous open white paper around it, and every edge dissolving into the paper in sparse broken strokes. Three-quarter front view: the Hero stands at the centre facing the dark passage at the right, the torch falling from the open left hand toward the floor; the right hand reaches across the front of the body to the sword at the left hip; the passage is solid black with two vague hunched shapes.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Human Hero in a hooded mantle and leather coat, weight on the back foot
* Left arm lowered in front of the body, fingers just opening, the torch half a foot below them, flame drawn as a few clean ink tongues
* Right hand crossing in front of the waist to draw a shortsword forward and up out of the one scabbard on the left hip, the blade half out
* Worn cellar stair behind, three or four steps
* Two indistinct hunched shapes in the black passage, outlined so they read in print
Must be shown exactly:
* One Hero only; the shapes stay vague
* Both arms stay in front of the body; no hand reaches behind the back
* Every figure has exactly two arms, two hands and two legs; shoulders, elbows, wrists and knees bend only the natural way, no limb twists behind the body or merges into another figure
* Every weapon and tool is one whole, correctly built object that is gripped by a visible hand, sheathed, or resting on a surface; nothing floats in the air
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* The Hero drops the torch and draws in one movement
A heartbeat of decision in the dark.
Do not draw:
* No lettering, numbers, dice, game tokens, or rulebook pages.
* No detailed Monster.
* Not the Undercroft or the Shadowed Under Passage.
* No glow effect around the torch.
```

## Climates (6)

Printed 3.3 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: Landscape that captures the climate's defining terrain, ruin and danger, with one or two tiny figures for scale. Composition reads as a vignette: the drawing is densest at its focal point and thins out into white paper toward irregular edges; no rectangular edge, no frame.

### 1. The Greenmark ✓

`ART-CLI-THE-GREENMARK`

```text
THE GREENMARK - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of the Greenmark, the great temperate forest of the surviving world, larger than the maps, where roots take roads and the forest does not recognize human law, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
A forest road at ground level in deep woods, roots lifting the paving, trees seeming to lean in; a moss-warm grove circle opens in the middle distance.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Ancient oaks with fluted trunks and heavy canopy
* Road slabs broken and swallowed by roots, a milestone half-buried and tilted
* Foresters' warning signs cut in bark as slashes and notches, not letters
* Upright hound-like shapes between trunks, dark and indistinct
* Grove-circle of standing mossy stones with low mist and a patch of bare paper where snow has melted
* Fern, bracken, fallen logs, toadstools
* Light shafts as blank paper between trunks
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"The Greenmark is the great temperate forest of the surviving world. It is larger than the maps. Roots take a road within a season and make the milestones useless. Travellers report trees shifting at the edge of sight, upright hounds between trunks, and grove-circles where moss stays warm after snow. Foresters cut warning signs into bark for those who know how to read them. The most common sign means turn back even if the voice ahead is your own. The forest is no wilderness. It is a country that does not recognize human law."
Ancient, watchful and indifferent to humans.
Do not draw:
* No people; the climate is the subject.
* Do not make the grove circle the focus: it is a small mossy ring of stones in the middle distance, so this is not the Whispering Grove scenario (standing stones in a clearing as the whole subject).
* Do not detail the hound shapes: barely seen silhouettes only.
* No letters in the bark signs.
```

### 2. The Ashlands ✓

`ART-CLI-THE-ASHLANDS`

```text
THE ASHLANDS - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of the Ashlands, the cities of the old empires after fire, law and death finished with them, where the city still knows how to count, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
Street-level one-point perspective down a soot-black avenue between collapsed forums and leaning temples, with a lit shrine lamp in the near corner.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Black-streaked pavement with ash drifts and cracked flagstones
* Collapsed forum colonnade, columns snapped and lying across the street
* Temple facade leaning over a plaza filled with soot
* Shrine niche with a small lit lamp and no worshippers
* Crypt entrance gaping where a forum floor gave way
* Far end: an armoured patrol silhouette crossing an old boundary line
* Ash drifts and thin falling flecks
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* A small file of armoured dead patrols far down the street, tiny
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"The Ashlands are the cities of the old empires after fire, law, and death finished with them. They are the hub of the world, and the imperial roads still run out from them like spokes from a shattered wheel. Forums have collapsed into crypts. Temples lean over plazas filled with soot. Streets run black across districts no living guide will enter twice. Lamps still burn in shrines whose gods have no congregation. Patrols still cross boundaries that vanished before the current Bastions had names. The Ashlands are rich because whole treasuries died under stone. They are feared because the city still knows how to count."
Austere, ordered and dead.
Do not draw:
* Not the Hollow Crown throne room or any scenario ruin.
* Do not detail the patrol: tiny silhouettes only.
* No inscriptions.
* Not a sunny scene; heavy overcast.
```

```text

* Cave-breath mist rising from a cellar floor crack

SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"The Undermarches begin under every place that claims to have a foundation, and run deepest beneath the broken mountains at the world's centre. Mines break into older shafts. Older shafts open into tunnels no pick made. Surveyors return with maps that disagree with themselves. Webs thick as rope choke the lower galleries, and the miners who cut them do not ask what spun them. Wells sour overnight. Cellars breathe in winter. Borders on the surface mean little below, where passages run under rivers, burial hills, and walls the Bastions call secure. Down in the true dark the deep elves keep their own houses and their own law, older than any empire and crueller than any court that ever sat above. The deep does not need to invade. The surface keeps falling into it."
Claustrophobic, uncanny, and too deep.
Do not draw:
* Not the Chasm Crossing, Crystal Harvest or Quake scenes.
* No deep elves visible.
* No glowing crystals.
* No readable map lettering.
```

Hearthhold sends parties north with oil, fire-charms, and instructions to leave tribute on the old road. No one agrees who first made that bargain. The snow covers ruin with clean hands. It heals nothing."
```text
Silent, bureaucratic cold.
```

`ART-CLI-THE-MIRE`

```text
THE MIRE - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of the Mire, the southeastern swamps where rivers give out, where bogs swallowed roads and the dead drift below the walkways, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.

* Plank walkway with missing boards
* Water that looks shallow but opens in a darker circle

* A lantern on a pole, unlit
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* A lone toll-ferryman poles a flat boat in the far distance, tiny
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Where the southern rivers gave out before the sea, the swamps inherited the rest, spreading across the world's south-eastern lowlands. Bogs swallowed roads. Mangroves grew through village roofs. Stilt-houses lean over water that looks shallow until it opens. Reedmarch survives by toll, ferry, leechcraft, and denial. The hag-courts hold the deep fen, and they keep older bargains than any Bastion will admit to honouring. Bells toll under the black water at the fen's heart, and every stilt-village knows the day its tribute falls due. Everyone there knows The Mire is rising. No one profits from saying it aloud. The dead drift below the walkways. Sometimes they drift back."
Damp, patient and quietly rising.
Do not draw:
* Not Reedmarch itself (no barges, council hall or toll bridges) and not Vossegar's cathedral.
* No horror close-ups: dead shapes stay ambiguous and faint.
* No lettering.
* Not the Blighted Heartwood or Titan fen scenes.
```

## Bastions (6)

Printed 3.3 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: The town or fortress seen from its approach road, with its defining feature unmistakable and tiny figures for scale. Composition reads as a vignette: the drawing is densest at its focal point and thins out into white paper toward irregular edges; no rectangular edge, no frame.

### 1. Greythorn ✓ ★

`ART-BAS-GREYTHORN`

```text
GREYTHORN - CLASSIC FANTASY PEN-AND-INK ILLUSTRATION
Create an exceptionally detailed, panoramic black-and-white pen-and-ink illustration of Greythorn, a fortified medieval forest town in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish. The artwork should resemble a meticulously hand-drawn illustration from a classic 1970s–1990s fantasy tabletop roleplaying game rulebook.
FORMAT AND WHITE VIGNETTE
Wide 3:2 landscape composition. Pure black ink on warm ivory-white paper, with no colour, lettering, borders, or decorative framing.
The illustration must have a natural, irregular, hand-drawn white vignette around all four edges. The artwork gradually dissolves into blank ivory-white paper through increasingly sparse pen strokes, broken outlines, diminishing crosshatching, and scattered stippling.
Leave approximately 5–8% clean white margin around the perimeter, with an additional organic transition zone. Avoid digital blur, artificial feathering, or hard rectangular cropping. Preserve crisp detail and strong contrast throughout the central composition.
COMPOSITION AND TOWN SCALE
View Greythorn from an elevated forested hillside, looking down across an ancient woodland road toward the fortified settlement.
Greythorn is a substantial medieval town of approximately 10,000 inhabitants, occupying a broad forest clearing roughly 1–2 km across. Its scale should resemble a significant medieval market town rather than a village, isolated fort, or castle.
The town occupies approximately 70–80% of the central composition's width. Hundreds of visible rooftops extend through successive layers of streets and buildings toward distant palisades, suggesting thousands of structures beyond those individually distinguishable.
The settlement follows gently rising terrain, with dense residential districts, commercial streets, workshops, and open civic spaces. Its distant buildings become progressively smaller and lighter, establishing convincing depth and urban scale.
Two enormous, ancient oak trees frame the foreground, their gnarled trunks, exposed roots, and spreading branches forming a natural canopy without obscuring the town.
A winding forest road leads across a weathered wooden bridge spanning a rocky stream toward the fortified main gate.
GREYTHORN'S FORTIFICATIONS
Greythorn's defensive perimeter consists primarily of tall sharpened timber palisades, reinforced earthworks, elevated wooden fighting platforms, and regularly spaced watchtowers.
A substantial timber gatehouse stands in the middle foreground, with two square defensive towers flanking an open entrance. Guards watch arriving travellers, while rangers patrol the battlements.
The fortifications are functional, weathered, and repeatedly repaired, reflecting a practical frontier settlement rather than an imperial capital.
THE TOWN INTERIOR
Beyond the walls, a dense and architecturally coherent medieval townscape spreads across the clearing.
Buildings consist predominantly of half-timbered houses, workshops, and warehouses with steep shingled or thatched roofs, exposed beams, narrow windows, wooden balconies, and irregular smoking chimneys.
Winding streets, courtyards, small squares, and crowded residential blocks create an organic medieval settlement.
Include several recognizable landmarks:

* A tall central wooden watchtower with a weathered banner.
* A substantial timber council hall.
* An exceptionally large military training yard with archery targets and practising rangers.
* A modest chapel and a small, understated arcane spire.
* A busy crossroads market with merchant stalls and wagons.
* The Hollow Quarter, a secluded lower district incorporating ancient trees, wooden walkways, and small bridges.

Integrate these landmarks naturally into the urban fabric without allowing individual buildings to dominate the overall townscape.
ANCIENT FOREST AND STANDING STONES
Dense primeval woodland surrounds Greythorn. Towering oaks, twisted branches, tangled roots, ferns, moss-covered boulders, and thick undergrowth create the impression of a settlement surviving within an ancient, powerful forest.
In the left foreground, beside the road, place several weathered standing stones carved with spirals and indistinct runes. Small ritual offerings of bundled branches, herbs, and woven baskets rest at their bases, marking the site of Greythorn's annual tithe to the forest.
Subtle supernatural details may reward close inspection, such as an indistinct woodland figure partially concealed among distant trees. Avoid prominent magical effects.
HUMAN ACTIVITY
Populate the roads, bridge, gatehouse, market, and training yard with numerous realistically scaled figures engaged in ordinary activity.
Include cloaked rangers carrying longbows, guards with spears, foresters carrying axes, merchants, burdened horses, supply wagons, apprentices, and townspeople.
Their activities should convey a prosperous but vigilant frontier settlement, without distracting from the architecture and landscape.
ARTISTIC STYLE AND ATMOSPHERE
The atmosphere is grounded, austere, mysterious, and subtly ominous. Greythorn is a resilient bastion of civilization maintained through vigilance and uneasy agreements with the ancient forest.
Use exceptionally fine traditional pen-and-ink rendering, with varied line weights, intricate crosshatching, stippling, textured bark, weathered timber grain, irregular stonework, and carefully constructed rooftops.
Strong foreground contrast and progressively lighter distant detail create atmospheric depth. Maintain accurate perspective, believable construction, and coherent urban geography.
The illustration must feel authentically hand-drawn rather than digitally rendered.
FINAL VISUAL REQUIREMENTS
Prioritize the expansive fortified town and its densely inhabited architecture, framed by monumental ancient oaks. The winding road, wooden bridge, gatehouse, and standing stones establish the foreground narrative.
Avoid elaborate stone castles, exaggerated fantasy towers, artificial symmetry, competing focal points, photorealism, painterly shading, smooth gradients, colour, typography, watermarks, and modern objects.
The finished artwork should be exceptionally detailed yet visually readable, with a convincing medieval urban scale, immersive forest atmosphere, and an organically fading white vignette suitable for seamless reproduction in a professionally printed fantasy rulebook.
```

### 2. Sablekeep ✓

`ART-BAS-SABLEKEEP`

```text
SABLEKEEP - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of Sablekeep, a fortified chapel-town built into the throat of a ruined imperial city where the Ashlands meet the eastern forest, ruled by the Sable Watch, which survives by burning, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
View from the straight imperial road looking up at the chapel-town climbing terraces in the throat of the ruined city; braziers burning at every gate, bridge and chapel window.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Terraced chapel-town in dark stone with narrow buttresses and a bell turret
* Lower terraces resting on the bones of an older temple, some arches oddly shaped, almost rib-like, but not clearly bones
* Consecrated braziers on iron stands at gates, bridges and windows, flames drawn as ink strokes
* Ruined imperial city walls and colonnades rising behind
* Eastern forest edge pressing on one flank
* Gate with a bridge over a dry cut and warrior-priests in dark cloaks on watch
* Smoke rising in thin lines
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* Warrior-priests tend braziers at the gate, small
* A column of travellers approaches on the road
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Sablekeep is a fortified chapel-town built into the throat of a ruined imperial city, where the Ashlands meet the eastern edge of the forest and the old roads run straight on into the dead empire. It survives by burning. Consecrated braziers stand at every gate, bridge, and chapel window. They are never permitted to go out. The lower terraces rest on the bones of an older temple, and the bones are not all the right shape."
Austere, fanatical vigilance against the dead city.
Do not draw:
* Not a standard castle; a chapel-town crowded on terraces.
* Do not show readable emblems of the Watch.
* No Hearthhold-like central fire or Deepwarden gate.
* No bones shown explicitly as skeletons.
```

```text
SOURCE TEXT
```

## Ancestries (11)

Printed 1.9 in wide · aspect 2:3. Family framing: Single full-length adventurer of this ancestry in travel-worn gear, three-quarter view. Isolated on white paper with only a small patch of ground and its cast shadow; no background scenery.

### 1. Human ✓

`ART-ANC-HUMAN`

```text
HUMAN - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of a Human adventurer, medium-sized, the plain short-lived 'striver' people who persist where steadier peoples fail, shown as a traveller in a Bastion's service, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
Full figure in three-quarter view mid-stride on a short patch of rutted track, walking staff planted ahead, head turned back over the shoulder toward unseen pursuit.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Layered road clothes: patched tunic, belted over-coat, hooded mantle pushed back
* Walking staff of plain ash, scuffed iron ferrule
* Short sword in a worn scabbard at the left hip
* Travel pack with bedroll and tin cup lashed on
* Ordinary unremarkable face, stubble, weathered skin, determined set jaw
* Mud-caked boots and frayed hem
* No horns, fur, scales or pointed ears: wholly ordinary proportions
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Humans are short-lived strivers who raise fragile things in a brutal world and call the result civilization. They learn fast, adapt faster, and persist in places that wear steadier peoples down to nothing."
Plain weathered resolve, a small life walking through a vast dying land.
Do not draw:
* No exotic features: the Human must look unmarked beside the other ancestries.
* No armour plate or heroic pose: this is a traveller, not a Fighter.
* Do not copy the Elf's long cloak or the Halfling's hood.
```

## Backgrounds (20)

Printed 1.6 in wide · aspect 1:1. Family framing: A small still life of the tools, garments and keepsakes of this background's trade; no figure, so it never resembles a class or ancestry portrait. Isolated on white paper; no background scenery. PHYSICAL PLAUSIBILITY (owner review 2026-10-13): every object rests on a surface, leans against a visible support, or hangs from a visible hook, strap, peg or nail, and it obeys gravity: nothing floats or juts out unsupported, cloth and rope hang downward, heavy things sit low, a balance scale tips toward its heavier pan. Each object is the true medieval European form of the thing named, drawn correctly: no modern items (fountain pens, wristwatches, zippers) and no East Asian weapons (no kunai, shuriken or ninja gear).

## Classes (12)

Printed 1.9 in wide · aspect 2:3. Family framing: Single full-length adventurer in an action-ready pose with class-typical arms and gear, three-quarter view. Isolated on white paper with only a small patch of ground and its cast shadow; no background scenery.

### 1. Fighter ✓

`ART-CLA-FIGHTER`

```text
FIGHTER - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of a Fighter, Martial class: drilled, line-holding soldier of fortune; packages of Shortsword, Targe, Ring Shirt, Dagger, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
Full figure in three-quarter view braced low with a small round targe held forward, shortsword drawn back over the hip, taking an unseen charge.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Ring shirt (mail of linked rings) over a padded arming coat
* Targe: small round shield with a central boss, edge dented
* Plain shortsword, single-handed, held ready
* Dagger on belt
* Open-faced helm or coif, no visor
* Stance planted wide, weight forward, knuckles white
* Scuffs and rust on mail, boots braced on a small patch of cracked flagstone
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"A Fighter is forged in places where hesitation gets people killed. Drill, repetition, and hard lessons carve in the instinct to hold ground when holding it is the only thing that matters. They have watched lines collapse, and they know to the heartbeat how long one can stand before it does."
Stoic resolve, the last thing between the line and collapse.
Do not draw:
* Not heavy plate: ring shirt only.
* Do not give it a two-handed weapon (that is the Berserker or Ravager package).
* No flourish or duel pose.
* No gate, wall or other scenery.
```

```text
* Longsword with a plain cross-guard, point resting in cracked flagstones
* Targe slung on the left arm or back
* Healer's kit pouch on the belt
* Bare-headed with cropped hair or a helm hung at the belt
* Strong shoulders squared, face composed
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"A Paladin is bound to a promise that does not bend, in an age that breaks promises for sport. Oaths weigh more than steel, and keeping one costs more than strength. Most let theirs go. A Paladin does not."
Unyielding vow, grave dignity.
Do not draw:
* Not the Cleric: no kneeling, no mace.
* No glowing sword or wings.
* No full plate.
* No doorway, arch or chapel.
```

## Basic Mode Heroes (24)

Printed 1.9 in wide · aspect 2:3. Family framing: Single full-length Hero standing alone, drawn whole from the top of the head to the soles of the feet, in a ready three-quarter pose that shows the listed ancestry, class, background and every listed item of equipment. Borderless and isolated on blank white paper with no background of any kind: only a few sparse strokes of ground and a short cast shadow directly under the feet.

### 1. Ironscar ✓

`ART-HER-IRONSCAR`

```text
IRONSCAR - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of Ironscar, a Dwarf Fighter with a Soldier background: a medium-sized, very broad, heavy shield-wall veteran, named for the old iron-hard scar that splits the face, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
Full figure in three-quarter view, braced low behind a heater shield held forward on the left arm, mace drawn back at the hip in the right hand. Tonal key: dark shield and helm against a pale coat. Silhouette: a wide square block.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Very broad shoulders, short thick limbs, human height but far heavier in build
* A deep old scar from brow to jaw through one cheek and the braided beard, drawn as one bold pale line
* Mail shirt (linked rings) drawn as a simple ringed texture on the chest and upper arms only, over a plain padded coat
* Dented open kettle helm with a wide brim, no visor
* Heater shield: flat-topped and pointed at the foot, its face divided into a solid black upper half and a plain white lower half, with a few dents
* Plain flanged mace with a short haft in the right hand
* Dagger sheathed at the belt, hilt clearly visible
* No cloak and no surcoat: bare mail and coat, belt and plain boots
Must be shown exactly:
* One mace (single-handed) and one heater shield, both visibly held
* A dagger sheathed at the belt
* Mail shirt of linked rings
Stoic, unbreakable, the last stone of the line.
Aim halfway between the two attached versions, with a clear preference for version 1's old-school pen-and-ink character. Use lively variable-weight dip-pen contours, rough brush accents, scratchy directional hatching, local crosshatching and dry-brush shadows. Recover version 1's tactile, hand-drawn materials without its indiscriminate distress or crowded texture. Balance this with version 2's readable solid blacks, open white areas, intact clothing, distinct silhouette and clearly constructed equipment. Use medium detail density: richer and rougher than version 2, quieter than version 1. Clothing is worn but maintained, with intact hems and occasional neat repairs. Each hero has individual facial features, clothing and material choices. Keep all required equipment legible at about two inches wide. Light from upper left. Pure black ink on white, no grey wash, colour or digital polish.
Do not draw:
* Not a plate knight: mail shirt only.
* No horned helmet.
* Not a comic dwarf: serious proportions, no oversized nose.
* No tattered, torn, ragged, frayed or fringed cloth anywhere: every hem and cloak edge is cleanly cut or neatly hemmed, with at most one or two small neat patches.
* No uniform all-over hatch mesh or stipple fields: use expressive local scratchy hatching and crosshatching on shadowed forms, balanced with solid blacks and open white highlights.
* No tint, tone or colour of any kind, including on foliage, skin and metal: pure black ink on white paper only.
* No background, scenery, wall, doorway, sky or second figure: the hero stands alone on bare white paper.
* No frame, border, panel box or vignette edge.
* No lettering, runes, name or caption anywhere, including on shields, scrolls and banners.
* Nothing cropped: the whole figure and every weapon are visible from the top of the head to the soles of the feet.
* Nothing touches the edge: keep at least 10% clean white margin on all four sides, and no stray specks, spatter or ground strokes outside the figure and its short shadow.
* No glow, aura, magical effect or speed lines.
Avoid photorealism, digital-render polish, clean vector-like line, stippled or noisy texture, all-over mechanical crosshatching, painterly shading, smooth gradients, grey wash, grey or coloured tint, airbrush, typography, readable runes, watermarks, signatures, modern objects, cartoon or anime proportions, tattered or ragged cloth, stray specks, cropped limbs and anything touching the image edge. The finished artwork is pure black ink on white, bold and readable at small size, with at least 10% clean white margin on every side, and prints crisply, like original ink art in an old-school fantasy rulebook.
```

## Equipment (13)

### 1. 7.6 Treasure Tables ✓

`ART-ITE-7-6-TREASURE-TABLES`

```text
7.6 TREASURE TABLES - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of Treasure in the Dying Lands is never clean: coin and relics come from hoards, vaults and the dead, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
Still life on a stone slab at slight elevation: a rotted purse spilling tarnished coins beside a skeletal hand, a mud-caked crown, a cracked reliquary and a salt-crusted signet ring.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Rotted leather purse with a frayed drawstring spilling coins
* Skeletal hand with finger bones curled near the coins
* Mud-caked crown with dull gems and a bent circlet
* Cracked reliquary box with a missing hinge
* Salt-crusted signet ring on a stone
* Coins hatched to feel dull, no readable stamps
* Cobweb, grave dirt and a spilled bone button
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Treasure in the Dying Lands is never clean. Coin comes from burial hoards, failed relief columns, chapel vaults, drowned roads, damned shrines, dead merchants, broken armies, and rooms sealed by people who feared being found. A purse is often the last record of a life. A crown may have passed through dirt, blood, ash, salt water, and another thief's stomach before it reaches a living hand."
Greedy and grave-soiled.
Ink it like old-school rulebook equipment art: a bold, rough contour of uneven weight around each object, heavy solid-black shadow shapes, and scratchy hatching or dry-brush drag that follows each material: grain on wood, dents and pits on metal, folds and wear on leather and cloth. High contrast, with few in-between textures. Each object reads as a clear silhouette first and detail second: no stipple fields, no uniform micro-texture, no over-rendered surfaces. Light falls from the upper left. Believable construction, worn and used. It must look drawn by hand with brush, pen and ink, not generated or rendered.
Do not draw:
* Not the Magic traditions still life or the melee weapon still life.
* No readable coin inscriptions.
* No glinting sparkle effects.
* No gore.
```

## Spell and power tiers (19)

Printed 2.6 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: Small scene vignette of the tradition's magic at work, densest at the caster and thinning into white paper toward irregular edges; no frame.

### 1. Tier 1 Arcane Spells (d20) ✓

`ART-TRA-TIER-1-ARCANE-SPELLS-D20`

```text
TIER 1 ARCANE SPELLS (D20) - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of Tier 1 Arcane Spells: the first lessons of any surviving academy (Arcane Bolt, Burning Hands, Ice Ray, Force Push, Phase Step), in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
A young apprentice in three-quarter view beside a scarred practice post, conjuring a small flame in one palm while a shimmer of force bends the air before the other hand; mid-height. Small vignette, densest at the caster and thinning into white paper.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Apprentice in a plain, too-large robe with chalk and ink stains
* Practice post with chopped and scorched marks
* Small flame drawn as a single clean ink flame cupped in the palm
* Air distortion drawn as bent parallel lines before the other palm
* A few cracked flagstones underfoot
* Anxious concentrated face, tongue-tip between teeth
* A battered wooden staff leaning on the post
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"The first lessons any surviving academy still teaches: force, fire, and the manipulation of what the eye reports. Apprentices roll for what their masters had time to pass on."
Nervous promise.
Do not draw:
* No lettering on tome pages or scrolls: marks only as abstract scribble.
* Not a grown Wizard: young, small and unsure.
* No big fireball or lightning.
```

### 2. Tier 2 Arcane Spells (d20) ✓

`ART-TRA-TIER-2-ARCANE-SPELLS-D20`

Small vignette, densest at the caster and thinning into white paper.
```text
SUBJECT DETAILS

* One scorched loose page with charred edges and unreadable marks, the only remnant of a burned library
* A small sphere of flame drawn as clean ink tongues hovering above the fresh sheet
* A few sparks drifting up from the sphere as flicked ink flecks
* Spilled ink pot and loose pages
* A satchel of rescued scraps at the feet
* Intent face, deep shadow under the brow
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Beyond the apprentice work lie the spells that bent the old empire's wars. Each was copied from a library that has since burned."
Careful, dangerous inheritance.
Do not draw:
* No lettering on tome pages or scrolls: marks only as abstract scribble.
* No bookshelves, library hall or burning building.
* No large explosion: the flame stays small.
```

```text
STEADFAST WORD - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of Steadfast Word, a Paladin's Tier 0 Orison: a mail-clad Paladin speaks one word and pulls a kneeling, Frightened companion back to their feet by a single clasped forearm, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
Closing tailpiece at the foot of a page, printed about 2.6 to 3.3 in wide and as short as 1.6 in: one clear focal subject with a strong silhouette in the middle of the frame, generous open white paper around it, and every edge dissolving into the paper in sparse broken strokes. Side view at knee height with clear space between the two bodies: the Paladin stands at the left, the companion kneels on one knee at the right; their only contact is one forearm-to-forearm grip in the middle of the picture.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Paladin in a ring shirt, targe strapped on the left forearm held at the side, mace hanging at the belt, mouth open on a single word
* Paladin's right hand and the companion's right hand gripping each other's forearms, the two arms forming one clear straight line
* Companion in a leather coat, pale and wide-eyed, rising from one knee, the free left hand pushing on the raised knee
* Dropped shortsword lying flat on the flagstones beside the companion
* A thin ring of short upright ink strokes on the ground around both figures
Must be shown exactly:
* Exactly two figures; the Paladin is lifting, not striking
* The two figures touch only at the clasped forearms; their other arms, hands and bodies stay separate
* Every figure has exactly two arms, two hands and two legs; shoulders, elbows, wrists and knees bend only the natural way, no limb twists behind the body or merges into another figure
* Every weapon and tool is one whole, correctly built object that is gripped by a visible hand, sheathed, or resting on a surface; nothing floats in the air
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* The Paladin speaks and pulls the companion upright
Plain courage passed from one hand to another.
Do not draw:
* No lettering, numbers, dice, game tokens, or rulebook pages.
* No halo, sunburst or radiant glow.
* Not the Tier 1 lantern-healing priest or the Tier 2 roadside shrine.
* No longsword drawn and no enemy in frame.
```

## Monsters (120)

Printed 1.9 in wide · aspect 1:1. Family framing: Single creature, full body, three-quarter view, in a characteristic menacing pose; a small human figure for scale only if the creature is Large or Huge. Isolated on white paper with only a small patch of ground and its cast shadow; no background scenery.

`ART-MON-GIANT-TARANTULA`

```text
GIANT TARANTULA - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of Giant Tarantula, Greenmark Skirmisher, small: a hand-span of bristled legs around a body the size of a war helm, a forest-floor venomous hunter, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.

High three-quarter view from above, looking down on the spider as it stands still on bare packed earth: the body in the centre and all eight legs radiating evenly around it like the spokes of a wheel, four on each side, every leg drawn whole from body to clawed foot and every foot planted on the ground, no leg raised, crossed, overlapping another or hidden by the body; fangs lowered; a discarded war helm lying well apart to one side for scale, touching no leg.
Size: Small: smaller than a human.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Exactly eight legs, jointed and tipped with small claws
* Body the size of a war helm, hairy abdomen and cephalothorax
* Bristle hairs suggested by short hatch flicks along the contours, not covering every surface
* Two large fangs with a bead of venom at the tips
* Cluster of eight eyes on a small raised bump
* Palps in front of the fangs
* Gripping toe-pads suggesting it can climb walls
* A rusted iron helm lying apart for scale
Must be shown exactly:
* Exactly eight legs
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"A hand-span of bristled legs around a body the size of a war helm, the giant tarantula stalks the Greenmark's leaf litter on stilted, deliberate steps. Their fangs weep a venom that turns a victim's muscles to wet rope. They spin no snares, preferring to run prey down and pin them where they fall. Foragers keep one eye on the canopy, because the largest specimens hunt from above and drop."
Patient, stilted menace in damp leaf litter.
Do not draw:
* No web: it spins no snares.
* Do not make it larger than a dog.
* No human features.
```

## Bosses (15)

Printed 2.6 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: The unique named boss shown with its distinguishing features in its own lair or setting, dramatic in scale; clearly an individual, not a typical member of a kind. Composition reads as a vignette that thins out into white paper toward irregular edges; no frame.

### 1. The Ancient Void Dragon ✓

`ART-MON-THE-ANCIENT-VOID-DRAGON`

```text
THE ANCIENT VOID DRAGON - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of the Ancient Void Dragon, a boss wyrm older than the Fall and perhaps its cause, whose scales drink light and whose breath unmakes substance, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
Night sky over a barren crag; the dragon is drawn mostly as negative space, a wyrm-shaped absence of untouched paper edged by fine contour lines, stars dimming around it. A broken watchtower stands small at the bottom for scale.
Size: Huge: towering many times a human's size.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Wyrm silhouette formed by blank paper bounded by thin broken contour lines
* Stars as small bare-paper specks in the black sky, thinning and vanishing near its outline
* Spreading wings and a long coiling neck implied by what the stars are missing
* Where the breath passes, the landscape is erased to bare white with abrupt edges
* Barren crag, snapped watchtower and a dry riverbed below
* Solid black used as the surrounding night sky, hatching to form the void contrast
* Peeling, fraying edges of substance along the breath
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Older than the Fall and perhaps its cause, the Ancient Void Dragon is a wound in the shape of a wyrm, their scales drinking the light from the air around them. Their breath neither burns nor freezes but unmakes, peeling away substance until nothing is left. Stars seem to dim where they pass. The wise do not speak their name, and the dead do not speak at all."
Cosmic dread; a hole in the world.
Do not draw:
* Not a normal rendered dragon with scales; absence is the subject.
* Not the Dracolich (bones) or Visath (ice).
* No glow or lens flare.
* No figures; at most one tiny broken tower.
```

## Scenarios (37)

Printed 3.3 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: The scenario's key place and threat at the moment before action, with small figures for scale. Composition reads as a vignette: the drawing is densest at its focal point and thins out into white paper toward irregular edges; no rectangular edge, no frame. Show the scenario's place and situation as the parties arrive; any named creature, guardian or boss appears only small, partial, distant or in silhouette, so the image is clearly distinct from that creature's bestiary portrait.

### 1. Scenario 1: Hunt the Wild Beast ✓

`ART-SCE-SCENARIO-1-HUNT-THE-WILD-BEAST`

```text
SCENARIO 1: HUNT THE WILD BEAST - GRITTY OLD-SCHOOL INK ILLUSTRATION
Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of Hunt the Wild Beast, a Greenmark scenario set in a Greythorn logging camp whose palisade timbers have been driven through and splintered by a Great Forest Boar that has made the deep wood its hunting ground, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
COMPOSITION
Wide, slightly raised view of a smashed logging camp at early morning, a game trail leading from its ruined palisade into dense forest in the middle distance. The beast is only a vast dim hump among the trees far back.
SUBJECT DETAILS
* Palisade of oak timbers snapped and splintered outward, one trunk driven through a sawpit shed
* Felled logs, stumps, an abandoned two-man saw and ox-yoke
* Cloven hoof furrows and torn earth leading into the trees
* A churned mud wallow glimpsed beyond the camp edge
* A narrow game trail, a pale gap in dense trunks
* Dead dire wolf lying near the camp gate, not gored
* Broken axe handles, a toppled log cart
HUMAN ACTIVITY
* Two small hunters with spears and boar-spears crouch at the camp edge reading the tracks
* One kneels to touch a splintered timber
* A watching raven on the palisade
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"Something out of the deep wood has taken Greythorn's logging camps for their hunting ground, and with them the timber the Bastion is built on. The first deaths were blamed on dire wolves. The next were found driven through splintered palisade timbers, which dire wolves do not manage. Their pelt, skull, and teeth would buy a season behind the walls, and the Council of Captains will pay either way to have the camps cutting again. Comfort and timber have sent both parties into the trees."
Brooding aftermath with the hunter still near.
Do not draw:
* Do not portray the boar: a barely seen shape only, no tusks in close-up.
* No boar carcass and no giant forest Avatar.
* Not the Bandit's Toll (this is a camp, not a trail barricade).
* No gore on the camp.
```

```text

Create a gritty, old-school black-and-white ink illustration of the Spider's Silk Route, a hidden Greenmark ravine where the Broodmother's daughters spin webs that hold steel like thread and fetch absurd prices in Bastion markets, in the dark fantasy world of Heraldry Skirmish, drawn the way a human illustrator inked the interior art of a late-1970s or 1980s fantasy rulebook or adventure module: brush, dip pen and India ink on paper, with the bite of a woodcut.
```

```text
* Large sheet webs strung between the rims, laden with dew-beaded strands

* A dagger and a rusted sword held motionless in a web strand as if in rope

HUMAN ACTIVITY

SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:

The spinners are the Broodmother's daughters, strayed up from the dark, and the place is on no honest map. That has not stopped a Greythorn merchant from selling the same path through the thorns to two parties at once."
Greed lured by beauty and dread.
Do not draw:
```

```text
SUBJECT DETAILS

* Emissary in fine but mud-stained robes, hands bound to a post, a satchel strap across their chest
* Forest-road reeve in half-armour with a longsword at the hip and a hand crossbow
* Clues in the foreground: a broken cart wheel, a snapped sealed document tube with no readable writing, torn cloth caught on a thorn, a cut rope hanging from a branch
* Tall oaks with small clearings and patches of undergrowth
* Dusk sky ruled lightly, crows returning to roost

* Two tiny watchers crouch in the ferns at the bottom edge
SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:
"An emissary vanished on the western forest road carrying documents sealed under two rival Bastion laws. One patron in Greythorn wants the courier found breathing. Another, further off, wants the satchel and does not ask after the courier. The deep wood has kept worse secrets, and the Hollow Quarter is not telling."
Tense, furtive espionage in the dark wood.
Do not draw:

* No readable seals or documents.
* No violence against the emissary.
* Not an adventurer: the emissary is a courier or diplomat.
```

## Stronghold rooms (11)

Printed 2.6 in wide · aspect 3:2. Family framing: Interior or architectural view of the described stronghold room. Composition reads as a vignette: the drawing is densest at its focal point and thins out into white paper toward irregular edges; no rectangular edge, no frame.

`ART-CAM-11-1-CAMPAIGN-OVERVIEW`

```text

SOURCE TEXT
Depict this faithfully and invent nothing that contradicts it:

It is a claim driven into a dying world with coin, fear, salvage, and stubborn labour. Its stones come from ruined shrines, abandoned watch-keeps, broken bridges, and walls that failed other people first. Its gates are paid for with burial gold, bounty coin, chapel debt, and the last wages of the dead."
Stubborn labour at the edge of ruin.

* Not a noble castle.
* No monsters.
* No readable plans or text.
```
